People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… honestly, I’m here because I scared myself. It’s not like I woke up one day and thought, “Oh, I should make an appointment,” it’s more that these thoughts have been getting harder to brush off. I keep thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. Like not in a dramatic way, I guess, but in this awful quiet way where it pops into my head and feels almost… relieving for a second, and then that scares me even more.

And sometimes after work, when I’m driving home from the clinic, I’ll catch myself thinking about what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I hate even saying that out loud. I work around animals and medical stuff all day, and I’m usually the person who keeps it together, so admitting that my mind is going there feels really frightening and embarrassing. But that’s what finally made me think I shouldn’t just keep pretending I’m fine.

elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: Earlier this month I felt almost electrically upbeat and then snapped at everyone over tiny things, like a misplaced medication cup or a change in the morning schedule, in a way that didn’t feel like my usual self.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: For several days I couldn’t sit still after work and kept pacing through the house, measuring rooms, making lists, and reorganizing cabinets long after everyone else had gone I honestly don’t know. I wish I had a neat timeline in my head, but I don’t. Before this past year… I’ve definitely had hard times, and times when I would say I was “down” or not myself, but whether it was nearly every day for two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can say confidently.

I tend to push through and not mark things as depression while they’re happening, if that makes sense. I just think, “I’m tired,” or “work is a lot,” or “this is just life.” So looking back, it’s kind of blurry. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just filling in the blanks, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m certain.

So… I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear memory of another distinct period like that before this past year.

I… no, I haven’t actually made an attempt. I haven’t done something where I can say I tried to end my life.

But what scared me enough to come in is that the thoughts are there more than I want to admit. I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n8\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F41.9, Z91.51\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… honestly, I’m here because I scared myself. It’s not like I woke up one day and thought, “Oh, I should make an appointment,” it’s more that these thoughts have been getting harder to brush off. I keep thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. Like not in a dramatic way, I guess, but in this awful quiet way where it pops into my head and feels almost… relieving for a second, and then that scares me even more.\n\nAnd sometimes after work, when I’m driving home from the clinic, I’ll catch myself thinking about what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I hate even saying that out loud. I work around animals and medical stuff all day, and I’m usually the person who keeps it together, so admitting that my mind is going there feels really frightening and embarrassing. But that’s what finally made me think I shouldn’t just keep pretending I’m fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days, really. It’s like… I don’t know how else to describe it except the color has drained out of everything. I can be in the house with noise and people and things going on around me, and it still feels like I’m behind glass or underwater or something.\n\nAnd I try so hard not to show it. I’ll keep moving, fold laundry, answer people normally, do whatever needs doing, but then I’ll end up in the laundry room crying because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing how hopeless I feel. It’s embarrassing, but yes… it’s most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches before, sure — who gets to 52 without some rough patches, right? But when you say “like this,” I don’t know if I can confidently say yes or no.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve felt low or overwhelmed, and I probably told myself it was stress or exhaustion or just needing to push through. I’m very good at explaining things away, honestly. But this particular feeling… the heaviness of it, and how much it’s been taking over my days… I don’t know. It feels different enough that it made me come here, which is not something I do easily.\n\nSo I guess my answer is: maybe in pieces, but I don’t know that I’ve had a period exactly like this before.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it did. It’s tied up with this whole situation of taking in our relative. I don’t even know how to summarize it without making it sound simpler than it is, because it’s not just one stressful day or one argument or something. It’s been this intense, constant up-and-down crisis around that change — trying to adjust, trying to do the right thing, trying not to fall apart.\n\nThat’s been going on for about a month now, I’d say. Around a month of feeling like everything is unstable and I’m always bracing for the next thing. I think that’s when this got really bad.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day like this?\n\nPatient: Yes… if I’m being honest, yes. I think I was trying to separate this past month out because it became so unbearable, but the truth is the depression didn’t just start then.\n\nIt’s been hanging over me for… maybe 11 months now? Almost a year, which sounds awful when I say it out loud. Before this recent situation made everything feel like it was collapsing, I was already feeling depressed nearly every day. I kept telling myself it was just a rough season or I was tired or I needed to get through the next thing, but it was there. This month just turned the volume up so high I couldn’t pretend around it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I wish I had a neat timeline in my head, but I don’t. Before this past year… I’ve definitely had hard times, and times when I would say I was “down” or not myself, but whether it was nearly every day for two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can say confidently.\n\nI tend to push through and not mark things as depression while they’re happening, if that makes sense. I just think, “I’m tired,” or “work is a lot,” or “this is just life.” So looking back, it’s kind of blurry. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just filling in the blanks, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m certain.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear memory of another distinct period like that before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, do you remember any time in your life when you were depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember one clearly, no. I mean… I’m hesitant to say “no” like I’m absolutely certain, because looking back over years of your life is messy, and I wasn’t exactly keeping track of my mood in a careful way.\n\nThere were definitely times I was unhappy or overwhelmed, and I’m sure there were stretches where I wasn’t myself, but whether I was depressed nearly every day for at least two full weeks before this past year… I just can’t point to a specific time and say, yes, that was it. It’s kind of blurred together in my memory.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear example before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made a suicide attempt?\n\nPatient: I… no, I haven’t actually made an attempt. I haven’t done something where I can say I tried to end my life.\n\nBut what scared me enough to come in is that the thoughts are there more than I want to admit. I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Not in a dramatic way, more like… I’m so tired that the idea of not having to do another day feels like relief, and then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nAnd sometimes when I’m driving home from the clinic, I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done it, but the fact that my mind even goes there… that really frightened me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I do feel tense a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious most days exactly. “On edge” maybe fits better sometimes, like I’m bracing for something or I can’t fully settle, but I haven’t really been tracking it day by day.

It’s not like I wake up and think, “I’m anxious today.” It’s more that I notice later that my shoulders have been up around my ears, or I’ve been kind of keyed up, or I’m having a hard time relaxing. But whether that’s most days… I don’t know. It might be, but I don’t want to overstate it if I’m just noticing it because you asked.

So I guess I’d say I’ve noticed some of that, but I’m not totally sure how often or how much it counts.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… honestly, I’m here because I scared myself. It’s not like I woke up one day and thought, “Oh, I should make an appointment,” it’s more that these thoughts have been getting harder to brush off. I keep thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. Like not in a dramatic way, I guess, but in this awful quiet way where it pops into my head and feels almost… relieving for a second, and then that scares me even more.\n\nAnd sometimes after work, when I’m driving home from the clinic, I’ll catch myself thinking about what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I hate even saying that out loud. I work around animals and medical stuff all day, and I’m usually the person who keeps it together, so admitting that my mind is going there feels really frightening and embarrassing. But that’s what finally made me think I shouldn’t just keep pretending I’m fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days, really. It’s like… I don’t know how else to describe it except the color has drained out of everything. I can be in the house with noise and people and things going on around me, and it still feels like I’m behind glass or underwater or something.\n\nAnd I try so hard not to show it. I’ll keep moving, fold laundry, answer people normally, do whatever needs doing, but then I’ll end up in the laundry room crying because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing how hopeless I feel. It’s embarrassing, but yes… it’s most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches before, sure — who gets to 52 without some rough patches, right? But when you say “like this,” I don’t know if I can confidently say yes or no.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve felt low or overwhelmed, and I probably told myself it was stress or exhaustion or just needing to push through. I’m very good at explaining things away, honestly. But this particular feeling… the heaviness of it, and how much it’s been taking over my days… I don’t know. It feels different enough that it made me come here, which is not something I do easily.\n\nSo I guess my answer is: maybe in pieces, but I don’t know that I’ve had a period exactly like this before.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it did. It’s tied up with this whole situation of taking in our relative. I don’t even know how to summarize it without making it sound simpler than it is, because it’s not just one stressful day or one argument or something. It’s been this intense, constant up-and-down crisis around that change — trying to adjust, trying to do the right thing, trying not to fall apart.\n\nThat’s been going on for about a month now, I’d say. Around a month of feeling like everything is unstable and I’m always bracing for the next thing. I think that’s when this got really bad.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day like this?\n\nPatient: Yes… if I’m being honest, yes. I think I was trying to separate this past month out because it became so unbearable, but the truth is the depression didn’t just start then.\n\nIt’s been hanging over me for… maybe 11 months now? Almost a year, which sounds awful when I say it out loud. Before this recent situation made everything feel like it was collapsing, I was already feeling depressed nearly every day. I kept telling myself it was just a rough season or I was tired or I needed to get through the next thing, but it was there. This month just turned the volume up so high I couldn’t pretend around it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I wish I had a neat timeline in my head, but I don’t. Before this past year… I’ve definitely had hard times, and times when I would say I was “down” or not myself, but whether it was nearly every day for two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can say confidently.\n\nI tend to push through and not mark things as depression while they’re happening, if that makes sense. I just think, “I’m tired,” or “work is a lot,” or “this is just life.” So looking back, it’s kind of blurry. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just filling in the blanks, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m certain.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear memory of another distinct period like that before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, do you remember any time in your life when you were depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember one clearly, no. I mean… I’m hesitant to say “no” like I’m absolutely certain, because looking back over years of your life is messy, and I wasn’t exactly keeping track of my mood in a careful way.\n\nThere were definitely times I was unhappy or overwhelmed, and I’m sure there were stretches where I wasn’t myself, but whether I was depressed nearly every day for at least two full weeks before this past year… I just can’t point to a specific time and say, yes, that was it. It’s kind of blurred together in my memory.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear example before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made a suicide attempt?\n\nPatient: I… no, I haven’t actually made an attempt. I haven’t done something where I can say I tried to end my life.\n\nBut what scared me enough to come in is that the thoughts are there more than I want to admit. I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Not in a dramatic way, more like… I’m so tired that the idea of not having to do another day feels like relief, and then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nAnd sometimes when I’m driving home from the clinic, I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done it, but the fact that my mind even goes there… that really frightened me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I do feel tense a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious most days exactly. “On edge” maybe fits better sometimes, like I’m bracing for something or I can’t fully settle, but I haven’t really been tracking it day by day.\n\nIt’s not like I wake up and think, “I’m anxious today.” It’s more that I notice later that my shoulders have been up around my ears, or I’ve been kind of keyed up, or I’m having a hard time relaxing. But whether that’s most days… I don’t know. It might be, but I don’t want to overstate it if I’m just noticing it because you asked.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’ve noticed some of that, but I’m not totally sure how often or how much it counts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Right now… I don’t think I have an intention to do it, no. I’m sitting here with you and I don’t feel like I’m about to act on it this minute.

But I don’t want to make it sound cleaner than it is, because what scared me enough to come in is that I keep having these thoughts like it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. And sometimes, especially driving home from the clinic, my mind goes to this place where I wonder what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t acted on that, but it’s frightening that the thought even shows up.

So… intention right now, no. But the thoughts are there, and they scare me.

I kept telling myself it was just a rough season or I was tired or I needed to get through the next thing, but it was there. This month just turned the volume up so high I couldn’t pretend around it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I wish I had a neat timeline in my head, but I don’t. No, I haven’t ever tried to kill myself in the past. I know I’m kind of repeating myself, but I want to be clear because the thoughts are what scared me, not that I’ve actually made an attempt before.

I kept telling myself it was just a rough season or I was tired or I needed to get through the next thing, but it was there. This month just turned the volume up so high I couldn’t pretend around it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ll keep moving, fold laundry, answer people normally, do whatever needs doing, but then I’ll end up in the laundry room crying because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing how hopeless I feel. It’s embarrassing, but yes… it’s most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches before, sure — who gets to 52 without some rough patches, right? But when you say “like this,” I don’t know if I can confidently say yes or no.\n\nThere have been times in my life when I’ve felt low or overwhelmed, and I probably told myself it was stress or exhaustion or just needing to push through. I’m very good at explaining things away, honestly. But this particular feeling… the heaviness of it, and how much it’s been taking over my days… I don’t know. It feels different enough that it made me come here, which is not something I do easily.\n\nSo I guess my answer is: maybe in pieces, but I don’t know that I’ve had a period exactly like this before.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it did. It’s tied up with this whole situation of taking in our relative. I don’t even know how to summarize it without making it sound simpler than it is, because it’s not just one stressful day or one argument or something. It’s been this intense, constant up-and-down crisis around that change — trying to adjust, trying to do the right thing, trying not to fall apart.\n\nThat’s been going on for about a month now, I’d say. Around a month of feeling like everything is unstable and I’m always bracing for the next thing. I think that’s when this got really bad.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day like this?\n\nPatient: Yes… if I’m being honest, yes. I think I was trying to separate this past month out because it became so unbearable, but the truth is the depression didn’t just start then.\n\nIt’s been hanging over me for… maybe 11 months now? Almost a year, which sounds awful when I say it out loud. Before this recent situation made everything feel like it was collapsing, I was already feeling depressed nearly every day. I kept telling myself it was just a rough season or I was tired or I needed to get through the next thing, but it was there. This month just turned the volume up so high I couldn’t pretend around it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I wish I had a neat timeline in my head, but I don’t. Before this past year… I’ve definitely had hard times, and times when I would say I was “down” or not myself, but whether it was nearly every day for two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can say confidently.\n\nI tend to push through and not mark things as depression while they’re happening, if that makes sense. I just think, “I’m tired,” or “work is a lot,” or “this is just life.” So looking back, it’s kind of blurry. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just filling in the blanks, but I also don’t want to say no like I’m certain.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear memory of another distinct period like that before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, do you remember any time in your life when you were depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember one clearly, no. I mean… I’m hesitant to say “no” like I’m absolutely certain, because looking back over years of your life is messy, and I wasn’t exactly keeping track of my mood in a careful way.\n\nThere were definitely times I was unhappy or overwhelmed, and I’m sure there were stretches where I wasn’t myself, but whether I was depressed nearly every day for at least two full weeks before this past year… I just can’t point to a specific time and say, yes, that was it. It’s kind of blurred together in my memory.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear example before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made a suicide attempt?\n\nPatient: I… no, I haven’t actually made an attempt. I haven’t done something where I can say I tried to end my life.\n\nBut what scared me enough to come in is that the thoughts are there more than I want to admit. I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Not in a dramatic way, more like… I’m so tired that the idea of not having to do another day feels like relief, and then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nAnd sometimes when I’m driving home from the clinic, I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done it, but the fact that my mind even goes there… that really frightened me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I do feel tense a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious most days exactly. “On edge” maybe fits better sometimes, like I’m bracing for something or I can’t fully settle, but I haven’t really been tracking it day by day.\n\nIt’s not like I wake up and think, “I’m anxious today.” It’s more that I notice later that my shoulders have been up around my ears, or I’ve been kind of keyed up, or I’m having a hard time relaxing. But whether that’s most days… I don’t know. It might be, but I don’t want to overstate it if I’m just noticing it because you asked.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’ve noticed some of that, but I’m not totally sure how often or how much it counts.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any intention to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t think I have an intention to do it, no. And sometimes, especially driving home from the clinic, my mind goes to this place where I wonder what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t acted on that, but it’s frightening that the thought even shows up.\n\nSo… intention right now, no. But the thoughts are there, and they scare me.\n\nDoctor: In the past, have you ever done anything to try to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not in the sense of actually doing something to end my life. I haven’t made an attempt before.\n\nWhat I’m trying to be honest about is that the thoughts have gotten scary. Like I said, what pushed me to come in is that I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then there are these moments driving home from the clinic where I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done that, and I haven’t acted on it, but the fact that my mind goes there feels really frightening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to kill yourself in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t ever tried to kill myself in the past. I haven’t done that, and I haven’t tried to end my life before, but having those thoughts show up feels really unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you felt anxious or worried more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel tense a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious most days exactly. “On edge” maybe fits better sometimes, like I’m bracing for something or I can’t fully settle, but I haven’t really been tracking it day by day.\n\nIt’s not like I wake up and think, “I’m anxious today.” It’s more that I notice later that my shoulders have been up around my ears, or I’ve been kind of keyed up, or I’m having a hard time relaxing. But whether that’s most days… I don’t know. It might be, but I don’t want to overstate it if I’m just noticing it because you asked.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’ve noticed some of that, but I’m not totally sure how often or how much it counts.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any intention to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t think I have an intention to do it, no. I’m sitting here with you and I don’t feel like I’m about to act on it this minute.\n\nBut I don’t want to make it sound cleaner than it is, because what scared me enough to come in is that I keep having these thoughts like it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. And sometimes, especially driving home from the clinic, my mind goes to this place where I wonder what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t acted on that, but it’s frightening that the thought even shows up.\n\nSo… intention right now, no. But the thoughts are there, and they scare me.\n\nDoctor: In the past, have you ever done anything to try to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not in the sense of actually doing something to end my life. I haven’t made an attempt before.\n\nWhat I’m trying to be honest about is that the thoughts have gotten scary. Like I said, what pushed me to come in is that I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then there are these moments driving home from the clinic where I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done that, and I haven’t acted on it, but the fact that my mind goes there feels really frightening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to kill yourself in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t ever tried to kill myself in the past. I know I’m kind of repeating myself, but I want to be clear because the thoughts are what scared me, not that I’ve actually made an attempt before.\n\nWhat made me come in is that I keep having this thought that it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes when I’m driving home from the clinic, I catch myself thinking, like… what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done that, and I haven’t tried to end my life before, but having those thoughts show up feels really unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you felt anxious or worried more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I’ve been worried, yes, but I don’t know if I can honestly say it’s been more days than not over the whole six months without thinking it through carefully.\n\nSome weeks it feels like my mind is constantly chewing on things, and other times I’m so focused on just getting through the day that I don’t even label it as anxiety. So… I guess I’d say I’ve had a lot of worry, but I’m not sure about the “more days than not” part.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever intentionally hurt yourself, even if you did not mean to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a deliberate way like cutting myself or burning myself or something like that. I haven’t done anything where I thought, “I’m going to hurt myself now,” even if I wasn’t trying to die.\n\nI’m hesitating because I don’t want to answer too quickly and leave something out, but no, as far as intentionally injuring myself, I don’t have a history of that. Yes. Very much so. That’s actually one of the things that’s been scaring me the most, because it doesn’t feel like normal “I’m busy” or “I’m tired of a hobby” kind of losing interest.

I haven’t touched my camera, and photography used to be something I’d almost automatically reach for. I haven’t really checked the bird feeders with any real interest either — I might notice them, but I don’t feel that little pull to watch or keep track of what’s out there. And I don’t really care what’s coming up in the garden, which is strange for me. Those things used to be how I felt like myself, if that makes sense. Like they were small but steady ways I stayed connected to life. So not caring about them feels frightening.

Yes… it’s changed a lot, but not in one clean direction, if that makes sense. My appetite has been kind of all over the place.

I don’t have a clear example before this past year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made a suicide attempt?\n\nPatient: I… no, I haven’t actually made an attempt. I haven’t done something where I can say I tried to end my life.\n\nBut what scared me enough to come in is that the thoughts are there more than I want to admit. I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Not in a dramatic way, more like… I’m so tired that the idea of not having to do another day feels like relief, and then I get scared that I’m even thinking that.\n\nAnd sometimes when I’m driving home from the clinic, I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done it, but the fact that my mind even goes there… that really frightened me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I do feel tense a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious most days exactly. “On edge” maybe fits better sometimes, like I’m bracing for something or I can’t fully settle, but I haven’t really been tracking it day by day.\n\nIt’s not like I wake up and think, “I’m anxious today.” It’s more that I notice later that my shoulders have been up around my ears, or I’ve been kind of keyed up, or I’m having a hard time relaxing. But whether that’s most days… I don’t know. It might be, but I don’t want to overstate it if I’m just noticing it because you asked.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’ve noticed some of that, but I’m not totally sure how often or how much it counts.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any intention to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t think I have an intention to do it, no. I’m sitting here with you and I don’t feel like I’m about to act on it this minute.\n\nBut I don’t want to make it sound cleaner than it is, because what scared me enough to come in is that I keep having these thoughts like it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. And sometimes, especially driving home from the clinic, my mind goes to this place where I wonder what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t acted on that, but it’s frightening that the thought even shows up.\n\nSo… intention right now, no. But the thoughts are there, and they scare me.\n\nDoctor: In the past, have you ever done anything to try to end your life?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not in the sense of actually doing something to end my life. I haven’t made an attempt before.\n\nWhat I’m trying to be honest about is that the thoughts have gotten scary. Like I said, what pushed me to come in is that I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then there are these moments driving home from the clinic where I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done that, and I haven’t acted on it, but the fact that my mind goes there feels really frightening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to kill yourself in the past?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t ever tried to kill myself in the past. I know I’m kind of repeating myself, but I want to be clear because the thoughts are what scared me, not that I’ve actually made an attempt before.\n\nWhat made me come in is that I keep having this thought that it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then sometimes when I’m driving home from the clinic, I catch myself thinking, like… what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. I haven’t done that, and I haven’t tried to end my life before, but having those thoughts show up feels really unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you felt anxious or worried more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I’ve been worried, yes, but I don’t know if I can honestly say it’s been more days than not over the whole six months without thinking it through carefully.\n\nSome weeks it feels like my mind is constantly chewing on things, and other times I’m so focused on just getting through the day that I don’t even label it as anxiety. So… I guess I’d say I’ve had a lot of worry, but I’m not sure about the “more days than not” part.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever intentionally hurt yourself, even if you did not mean to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a deliberate way like cutting myself or burning myself or something like that. I haven’t done anything where I thought, “I’m going to hurt myself now,” even if I wasn’t trying to die.\n\nI’m hesitating because I don’t want to answer too quickly and leave something out, but no, as far as intentionally injuring myself, I don’t have a history of that. The scary part has been the thoughts, not me actually doing something to my body.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much so. That’s actually one of the things that’s been scaring me the most, because it doesn’t feel like normal “I’m busy” or “I’m tired of a hobby” kind of losing interest.\n\nI haven’t touched my camera, and photography used to be something I’d almost automatically reach for. I haven’t really checked the bird feeders with any real interest either — I might notice them, but I don’t feel that little pull to watch or keep track of what’s out there. And I don’t really care what’s coming up in the garden, which is strange for me. Those things used to be how I felt like myself, if that makes sense. Like they were small but steady ways I stayed connected to life. So not caring about them feels frightening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a big change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s changed a lot, but not in one clean direction, if that makes sense. My appetite has been kind of all over the place.\n\nLately, though, it’s mostly that I don’t really eat. I’ll have coffee, maybe pick at some toast, and then I’ll get through a whole clinic shift and realize at the end that I’ve barely eaten anything. It’s not always that I’m deliberately avoiding food — it’s more like food just doesn’t register properly, or nothing sounds worth the effort, and then suddenly it’s hours later."
